**Tool · Write** `<repo>/NOTES.md`
```
# Notes

## Decisions

**1. Amounts: exact integers (`bigint`) in the asset's smallest unit.** Every API string is parsed strictly
(the API's own format, at most `decimals` places) into `value × 10^decimals`. Comparisons, fee, receive
amount and sums are integer math; the canonical string is rebuilt for the request (`src/lib/amount.ts`).
*Why:* ETH has 18 decimals. `Number("12.345678901234591676")` is `12.345678901234592`, so "Max" would send
a different value than `available`. *Alternatives:* a decimal library (an extra dependency; decimal.js
defaults to 20 significant digits and can round silently); JS `number` (wrong for this API).
USD is display-only. Each value is computed exactly, summed, and rounded **once** to cents (half away
from zero). Balance amounts are cut toward zero for display with a "…" (they never overstate). The review
screen shows exact values, and Activity shows the API strings verbatim.

**2. Balance right after a claim: overlay, reconciled by `asOf`.** After a confirmed 201, the app moves the
amount from available to claiming (or out, once completed), but only for snapshots **strictly older**
than the event. Equal timestamps are ambiguous, so the overlay stays one snapshot longer. Once a snapshot
with `asOf > createdAt` (or `> updatedAt`) arrives, server numbers are used as they are. A failed claim
gets no overlay and triggers an immediate refetch. While a claim is in flight, Home shows a banner and
re-fetches every 5 s (`src/features/claim/overlay.ts`, tested).
*Alternatives:*
- Server snapshot plus a banner: honest, but `available` looks unchanged for up to 15 s, which leads to "I claimed again".
- Naive optimistic update: a refetch returns a pre-claim snapshot, so the balance drops and then jumps back up.

The `asOf` rule fixes both complaints.

**3. Unknown result (504, timeout, network error, 5xx): replay the same Idempotency-Key.** The key and
body are written to storage (scoped to the token) **before** the first request. On an unknown outcome
the app replays the same key automatically 3 times (1 s / 2 s / 4 s; a 429's `retryAfterMs` wins), showing
"Confirming your claim…". After that, the user gets "It may have gone through. Don't submit it again"
and a **Check again** button. That asset can't be claimed until it's resolved, and a reload resumes the
check. On a replay:
- 400 means the claim was never created: the asset unblocks and the user can edit the amount.
- 409 is our bug: it is shown and never retried.

*Seen live:* my test claim got a 504 after 3.4 s, and the first replay returned **200** (the original
claim). Activity shows exactly one claim. *Rejected:* treating it as failed and retrying with a new key
(duplicate claim); matching Activity rows by amount and time (a guess).

**Others.**
- **Token.** Kept in `localStorage` and checked with `GET /v1/balances` before saving. Any 401 returns to the token screen. Changing the token wipes the token-scoped claim data, and balances live in memory only. Trade-off: a
```

````
# CashMine practical task

Expo (React Native, TypeScript) app delivered as a web app, designed for a ~390 px phone screen.

- Live: _link_
- Decisions and known issues: [`NOTES.md`](NOTES.md)
- AI conversations: [`ai-logs/`](ai-logs/)

## Run locally

```bash
yarn install
npx expo start --web
yarn test
```

On first open the app asks for your API token. It is kept in this browser (`localStorage`) and can be changed with **Token** in the header.

Optional, for calling the API from a terminal: `cp .env.example .env.local`, fill in the values, then `bash scripts/api.sh GET /v1/balances`.

## What works

- **Token gate**: asks for the token, checks it, stores it in the browser, lets you change it; a 401 anywhere brings you back to it.
- **Balances**: per-asset available / pending (may be reversed) / claiming, USD total and "claimable now", snapshot age, loading, error with retry, refresh.
- **Claim**: pick asset, amount or Max, destination; live validation against the API rules; fee and receive amount; review and confirm; status tracking until completed or failed. Balances include the claim right away (reconciled with the server snapshot). Unknown results are replayed with the same Idempotency-Key, so a claim is never created twice.
- **Activity**: paginated history of accruals, reversals and claims.
- **Tests**: `yarn test`, covering amount parsing, validation, fee, totals and the balance-after-claim overlay.

The API base URL lives in `src/api/config.ts`.
````

The 0.01 ETH test claim worked end to end, and it hit Decision 3 for real.

**What happened with the claim:**
1. The first `POST /v1/claims` returned **504 after 3.4 s**, so the outcome was unknown.
2. The app replayed **the same Idempotency-Key** once. The server answered **200, not 201**, meaning it returned the original claim: the 504'd request *had* created it.
3. Polling followed `clm_5f0b5d5d2432` to **completed**, with 0.0088 ETH sent to the Wallet. The fee and receive amount shown are the server's own values.
4. On Home, ETH `available` dropped by exactly 0.01 and didn't bounce, and the banner cleared once a newer snapshot arrived. Activity shows **exactly one** claim of −0.01 ETH, so no duplicate.

Before submitting, the validation messages for `.5`, `0.005` and 19 decimal places were all correct.

**Since then:**
- **Activity** is committed (`de37634`). Pagination works through to "No more activity" (28 items). Page 2 hit a 503 and the automatic GET retry handled it.
- **NOTES.md** is drafted (`52a56ab`). It covers the three decisions with what was rejected and why, plus the token, USD-total and destination assumptions. The "Working with AI" section points to phrases you can search for in `ai-logs/`, and there's a known-issues list. It's written in the first person for you, so edit anything that isn't how you'd put it. At ~990 words it's on the long side of "about a page".
- **README.md** has the run steps, `yarn test`, and what works.

**What I need from you:**
1. **Deploy**:
```bash
npx expo export --platform web
```
```bash
npx eas-cli@latest deploy --prod
```
Then paste me the URL. I'll put it in the README and check that reloading on `/activity` works with `single` output on EAS Hosting. That deep-link check is the one thing not verified yet.
2. **Export this session into `ai-logs/`**, redacted. The token never appeared in this conversation, and the claim-status URLs in the browser logs already show the key as `REDACTED`.
3. **Push to the public GitHub repo** before 14:00. `git push` is on the confirm list, so either run it yourself or tell me to.

```diff
          }}
        />
      )}
      {item.phase === "error" && (
        <Button
          title="Dismiss"
          variant="secondary"
          onPress={() => {
            dismiss(item.key);
            goHome();
          }}
        />
      )}
```

The duplicate Home is fixed and committed as `769bff8`. Every path I tested now ends with one Home, and typecheck, lint and the 43 tests pass. The live site still has the old build until you redeploy.

**Cause, confirmed by reading the navigation stack.** You were right that it's `replace("/")` with Home already below, but it came from a different call. The normal "Back to balances" (`goHome`) was fine: in the app, `canGoBack()` was true and `back()` popped to one Home. The duplicate came from the **"This claim is no longer tracked"** branch. Its button always called `router.replace("/")`, which turned `[index, claim-status]` into `[index, index]`: two Homes, the top one with a ← arrow. You land on that branch right after **Dismiss** on an errored claim, or from a status link this device doesn't know. I reproduced it in the browser before changing anything.

**Fix:**
- **Back to balances:** both buttons now call `router.dismissTo("/")`. In this expo-router version it pops back to the Home already in the stack. Only when there's no Home, as on a direct reload of `/claim-status`, does it fall back to replacing the screen, which is the one case you wanted `replace` kept for.
- **Edit amount:** it had the same bug. If you opened the status screen from the claim form's "View claim" banner, `replace("/claim")` stacked a second Claim. It now uses `dismissTo("/claim", { asset, amount })`, and the form restarts when it gets new params, so the amount you're fixing is prefilled.
- **Dismiss:** it now also returns to Home.
- **The other navigation calls are safe:** Home pushes to Claim, Activity and the status screen, and Confirm replaces the form with the status screen.

**What I checked in the browser:** the stack after every path in the table above. To walk through Confirm without making another real claim, the claim request was faked to return a 400 inside the page only. The debugging hook I added to read the stack is removed and wasn't committed.

**Not checked:** a real phone. If the left-edge peek still shows up on iOS Safari after the redeploy, it's probably Safari's own swipe-back preview, not a second Home.

To ship the fix:
```bash
npx expo export --platform web
```
```bash
npx eas-cli@latest deploy --prod
```

```
# Notes

## Decisions

**1. Amounts are `bigint` counts of the asset's smallest unit.** Amount strings from the API are parsed
strictly into `value × 10^decimals`, using the API's own format and at most `decimals` places. Comparisons,
fee, receive amount and sums are integer math. *Why:* ETH has 18 decimals, and
`Number("12.345678901234591676")` is `12.345678901234592`, so Max would send a different value than
`available`. *Alternatives:* a decimal library (an extra dependency, and decimal.js silently rounds at 20
significant digits by default) or `number` (wrong for this API). USD is display-only: summed exactly,
rounded once.

**2. Balance after a claim: the app adjusts it locally until the API snapshot catches up.** After a
confirmed 201, the amount moves from available to claiming at once. The adjustment applies only to
snapshots taken strictly before the claim; one with `asOf > createdAt` replaces it (equal timestamps are
ambiguous). A failed claim gets no adjustment, and balances are refetched. *Alternatives:*
- Raw snapshot plus a banner: available looks unchanged for up to 15 s, which leads to "I claimed again".
- Naive optimistic update: a stale refetch undoes it, so the balance "dropped, then jumped back up".

Comparing timestamps avoids both.

**3. Unknown result: send the same request again with the same Idempotency-Key.** The key and body are
stored (per token) before the first request. On a 504, timeout, network error or 5xx, the app resends them
up to 3 times (1/2/4 s apart), then shows "It may have gone through" with *Check again*. That asset can't
be claimed until it's resolved, and a reload resumes. If a resend gets a 400, the claim was never created.
A 409 is our bug and is not retried. *Rejected:* retrying with a new key (risks a duplicate claim) and
matching Activity rows by amount and time (a guess). *Seen live:* my test claim got a 504; the resend
returned 200 with the original claim, and Activity shows one claim.

**Assumptions.**
- The USD total is available + pending + claiming. Pending is labelled "may be reversed".
- The token is kept in `localStorage`, so any script injected into the page could read it.
- Claim data is scoped to the token and wiped when it changes.

   - checkpoints at 13:20 and 13:40
   - claim state scoped to the token

   Search: "We're over budget".
2. **Wrong or risky output.**
   - The claim screen let two taps in one frame call `submit()` with two different keys, which means two
     real claims. The AI caught this in its own review and added a ref guard. Search: "Double-tap race".
   - It also chained `tsc ; … ; git commit`, so a commit with a failing typecheck landed (`9406882`). I
     caught it in the command output.
3. **Pushing back.** I accepted its options for
```

```
# Notes

## Decisions

**1. Amounts are `bigint` counts of the asset's smallest unit.** The app parses amount strings strictly
into `value × 10^decimals`, so comparisons, fee, receive amount and totals are exact integer math.
*Why:* ETH has 18 decimals, and `Number("12.345678901234591676")` gives `12.345678901234592`, so Max
would claim a different amount than `available`. *Alternatives:* a decimal library (an extra dependency,
and decimal.js silently rounds at 20 significant digits) or plain `number` (wrong here).

**2. Right after a claim, the app moves the amount from available to claiming locally.** The API's
balance snapshot can be up to 15 s old. So the local change applies only while the snapshot is older
than the claim (`asOf ≤ createdAt`, since equal timestamps are ambiguous). After that the server numbers
win. A failed claim gets no local change, and balances are refetched. *Alternatives:* showing the raw
snapshot with a banner (the balance looks unchanged, so users claim again), or a naive optimistic update
(a stale refetch undoes it, so the balance drops and then jumps back up). Comparing timestamps avoids both.

**3. When a claim's result is unknown, the app resends it with the same Idempotency-Key.** The key and
request are saved before the first send. After a 504, timeout, network error or 5xx, the app resends up
to 3 times (1/2/4 s apart). Then it shows "It may have gone through" with a *Check again* button, and
blocks claiming that asset until the result is known. A resend that gets a 400 means the claim was never
created; a 409 means a bug in the app, so it isn't retried. *Rejected:* retrying with a new key (risks a
duplicate claim) or matching Activity rows by amount (a guess). *Seen live:* a 504, then the resend
returned the original claim, and Activity shows exactly one claim.

**Assumptions:**
- The USD total is available + pending + claiming, and pending is labelled "may be reversed".
- The token lives in `localStorage`, so a script injected into the page could read it.
- Saved claim data belongs to one token and is wiped when the token changes.

## Working with AI (search `ai-logs/` for the quoted phrase)

1. **A prompt that missed:** the first plan added up to ~2 h for a ~1 h 35 slot. I re-scoped it: merged
   steps, tests written next to `amount.ts`, checkpoints, claim state tied to the token. ("We're over budget")
2. **Wrong or risky output:** the claim screen allowed two taps in one frame to submit with two different
   keys, which is two real claims. The AI found this in its own review and added a guard ("Double-tap
   race"). It also chained `tsc ; … ; git commit`, so a commit that failed type-checking landed
   (`9406882`); I caught it in the command output.
3. **Pushing back:** I accepted its proposals for decisions 2 and 3 but changed them: strict timestamp
   comparison, no local change for failed claims, no retry on 409, a 3-resend cap. ("C and A, with tweaks")

## Not done / known issues

- Tested at 390 px in
```

```
## Decisions

**1. Amounts are `bigint` counts of the asset's smallest unit.** Amount strings are parsed strictly
into `value × 10^decimals`, so comparisons, fee and totals are exact integer math. *Why:* ETH has 18
decimals, and `Number("12.345678901234591676")` is `12.345678901234592`, so Max would claim a different
amount than `available`. *Alternatives:* a decimal library (an extra dependency; decimal.js rounds at 20
significant digits) or plain `number` (wrong here).

**2. After a claim, the app moves the amount from available to claiming locally.** The balance snapshot
can be up to 15 s old, so the local change applies only while `asOf ≤ createdAt` (equal timestamps are
ambiguous). After that the server's numbers win. A failed claim gets no local change and triggers a
refetch. *Alternatives:*
- Raw snapshot plus a banner: the balance looks unchanged, so users claim again.
- Naive optimistic update: a stale refetch undoes it, so the balance drops and then jumps back up.

**3. If a claim's result is unknown, the app resends it with the same Idempotency-Key.** The key is saved
before the first send. After a 504, a timeout or a 5xx, the app resends up to 3 times (1/2/4 s), then
shows "It may have gone through" with *Check again*. That asset stays blocked until the result is known.
- A 400 on a resend means no claim was created.
- A 409 means a bug in the app, so it is not retried.

*Rejected:* a new key (risks a duplicate claim) and matching rows in the activity history by amount (a
guess). *Seen live:* a real 504; the resend returned the original claim, and there was exactly one claim.

**Assumptions:**
- The USD total is available + pending + claiming; pending is labelled "may be reversed".
- The token is kept in `localStorage`, so a script injected into the page could read it.
- Claim data belongs to the token and is wiped when the token changes.

## Working with AI (search `ai-logs/` for the quoted phrase)

1. **A prompt that missed.** The first plan took ~2 h for a ~1 h 35 slot, so I re-scoped it. I merged
   steps, moved tests next to `amount.ts`, added checkpoints and tied claim state to the token. ("We're
   over budget")
2. **Wrong or risky output.** Two taps in one frame could submit with two different keys, which is two
   real claims. The AI caught this in its own review and fixed it ("Double-tap race"). It also chained
   `tsc ; … ; git commit`, so a commit that failed the type check landed (`9406882`). I caught that one
   in the output.
3. **Pushing back.** I accepted its proposals for decisions 2 and 3 but changed four things: a strict
   timestamp comparison, no local change for failed claims, no retry on 409, and a cap of 3 resends.
   ("C and A, with tweaks")

## Not done / known issues

- Tested at 390 px in a desktop browser, not on a phone. Light theme only.
- `ClaimsProvider` has no automated tests. Next: test it against a mocked API covering 504→200, 400, 409
  and three failures in a row.
- Tabs don't share clai
… [truncated 257 chars]
```

I found no must-fix bugs in money, claim or token handling. Tests (43/43), `tsc` and `expo lint` all pass. Nothing was changed.

**What I verified:**
- **No floats in money math.** The only `Number`/`Math.round` calls convert seconds (`client.ts:46`, `useNow.ts:14`).
- **Amounts follow the API rules.** Parsing uses the API's format and `decimals`, `min ≤ amount ≤ available` is enforced, Max is exact, and fee/receive are integer math. The USD total is summed exactly and rounded once.
- **The claim flow holds up:**
  - the Idempotency-Key is written to storage before sending (`ClaimsProvider.tsx:61`)
  - 3 automatic resends (1/2/4 s) on 504/timeout/5xx/429/network errors
  - 400 means "not created" and 409 is never retried
  - a double tap can't submit twice
  - polling stops once a claim settles
  - claim data is per-token and wiped on token change
  - the balance overlay only applies while `asOf ≤ createdAt`
- **The token** isn't in `src`, `dist` or the commits. In `ai-logs/` the only matches are placeholders and commands. A 401 returns to the token screen, and changing the token works.
- **The live build has the duplicate-Home fix.** Its `claim-status` chunk contains `dismissTo("/")` and no `replace("/")`.
- **NOTES/README match the code and logs.** Commits `9406882` and `5529826` exist. "We're over budget" (7 hits), "Double-tap race" (5) and "C and A, with tweaks" (7) are all in the Claude Code export, and both `ai-logs` files are committed.

**Nice-to-have, by severity:**
1. **If the browser blocks storage, the key isn't saved but the claim is still sent** (`src/features/claim/ClaimsProvider.tsx:61`, `src/lib/storage.ts`). After a reload, a claim with an unknown result would be forgotten and the asset unblocked. Fix: make `writeJson` return false and refuse to submit with a message. This is the one closest to a real gap, but it only matters where storage is blocked.
2. **A malformed `usdPrice` would crash the whole screen** (`src/features/balances/model.ts:22`). The price is parsed while rendering, not when balances load. Fix: parse it in `toBalances` so it shows the error-and-retry state instead.
3. **The Refresh button flashes a spinner every 5 s while a claim is in progress** (`src/app/index.tsx:32` and `:73`). Fix: show the spinner only for a manual refresh.
4. **Saving a token can spin for up to ~45 s if the API is down** (`src/features/token/TokenScreen.tsx:28`), because the check uses the GET retries. Fix: check once, with no retries.
5. **No empty state on Home** (`src/app/index.tsx:45`): with no balances it shows "$0.00" and no rows. Fix: add a "No payback yet" message.
6. **Unused export** (`src/api/client.ts:29`): `isOutcomeUnknown` is never used. Fix: delete it.

**Token check for you to run.** The leak check in your second chat didn't actually run: the token variable was empty (`ai-logs/claude-chat-…:925`). Your `.env.local` may hold the token in `API_AUTH_HEADER` rather than `API_TOKEN`. This command reads `.env.local` in your own terminal and prints only counts:

```bash
bash -c 'set -a; . ./.env.local; T="${API_TOKEN:-${API_AUTH_HEADER##* }}"; [ -n "$T" ] || { echo "token var not found"; exit 1; }; echo "history: $(git log -p --all | grep -cF -- "$T")"; echo "files: $(grep -rlF -- "$T" --exclude-dir=node_modules --exclude-dir=.git --exclude=".env*" . | wc -l)"'
```

Both numbers should be 0.

My recommendation is to ship as is. All six items touch working code, and anything beyond item 6 would need a redeploy with about 20 minutes left. If you want them on record, they can go into NOTES known issues without any code change.
